| C-01 | claude | Slice 01 adds a new runtime dependency (&#96;jsonschema&gt;=4.18,&lt;5&#96;) to &#96;pyproject.toml&#96; for offline JSON-Schema validation in &#96;src/artifact_models.py&#96;, but the pre-existing invariant test &#96;tests/test_cli.py::test_declared_python_floor_matches_standard_library_toml_requirement&#96; still asserts &#96;dependencies == []&#96;, so the bound orchestrator attestation for fingerprint f30b04cebf94756b25051f21bd5590a80075607da1c8e34685f17eb44b9ea028 is FAIL (1 failed / 713 passed). The slice must not merge an unreconciled dependency-policy break: either reconcile the "stdlib-only/portable" invariant explicitly (with the guard test updated in the same, reviewed change) or replace &#96;jsonschema&#96; with a stdlib-only Draft-2020-12 validator so the declared zero-dependency contract holds. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: Runtime &#96;jsonschema&#96; dependency removed; &#96;src/artifact_models.py&#96; now implements an offline, self-checking Draft-2020-12 subset validator with no third-party dependency; the bound attestation for fingerprint 6ce9f6f31673ac0333e7da8a4752ab18805ac64c803e298640eab9a2d0a60aba shows both the full suite (714 passed/0 failed) and the exact previously-failing guard test (&#96;tests/test_cli.py::test_declared_python_floor_matches_standard_library_toml_requirement&#96;) passing, restoring the zero-dependency invariant. |
| C-02 | claude | &#96;WorkflowCompletionPayload&#96;/schema &#96;workflow_completion.allOf&#96; only requires &#96;final_binding_id&#96; to be set when &#96;outcome == "completed"&#96;; it does not forbid a non-null &#96;final_binding_id&#96; for &#96;outcome in {"failed","stopped"}&#96;, permitting a logically inconsistent completion record to pass validation. This is acceptable to defer given cross-record invariants are explicitly scoped to Slice 02 (append-only store), but should be closed with an explicit negative-case test once that store exists. | OBSERVATION | angenommen | erledigt: Acceptance test satisfied: &#96;failed&#96;/&#96;stopped&#96; &#96;WorkflowCompletionPayload&#96; records carrying a non-null &#96;final_binding_id&#96; are now rejected at the store boundary (&#96;test_store_rejects_non_success_completion_with_binding&#96;, plus the symmetric scan-time invariant in &#96;_validate_store_invariants(persisted=True)&#96;), matching the negative-case acceptance criterion I set in Slice 01. |
| C-03 | claude | &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; can raise an exception (e.g., a transient error during the mandatory post-publish re-scan/head-cache refresh) after the record bytes are already durably and atomically published to &#96;records_dir&#96;, and this "durable-but-reported-as-failed" retry contract is exercised by one crash-recovery test but is not documented as an explicit API guarantee on &#96;put()&#96;. Slice 03 (dual-write/semantic equality) will depend on exactly this guarantee to decide whether to retry vs. treat an exception as "nothing was written," so an undocumented/underspecified contract here risks a double-write or a lost-write assumption once callers are wired in. | OBSERVATION | angenommen | erledigt: Acceptance criteria fully met in this diff: &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; now carries an explicit docstring contract ("An exception does not prove that nothing was written... callers must treat every exception as possibly already persisted and may safely retry the identical logical call"), and &#96;tests/test_artifact_store.py::test_scan_rejects_persisted_non_success_completion_with_binding&#96; directly exercises the &#96;persisted=True&#96; scan branch via a hand-written envelope that bypasses &#96;put()&#96;, matching my Slice-02 acceptance test verbatim. Closing as originally reported by me. |
| C-04 | claude | &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver._persist_structured_baseline()&#96; (invoked unconditionally, uncaught, from every &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call — i.e. every rebind/resume within a work unit) derives the &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96; idempotency key solely from &#96;unit.work_unit_id&#96; (&#96;idempotency_key=f"work-unit:{unit.work_unit_id}"&#96;), while the payload it constructs embeds &#96;round_number=unit.round_number&#96;. Elsewhere in the same diff (&#96;persist_codex_contract&#96;'s &#96;logical=f"agent-{unit.work_unit_id}-{state.current_step.value}-{unit.round_number}"&#96;, and &#96;persist_review_contract&#96;'s explicit &#96;round_number&#96; parameter used in its own idempotency key) the codebase treats &#96;round_number&#96; as a value that legitimately changes across multiple rounds within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96; (repair/retry/second-review cycles). The first &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call for round 1 durably publishes &#96;WorkUnitPayload(round_number=1,...)&#96; under key &#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;&#96;. As soon as the same work unit advances to round 2 and &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; fires again, &#96;ArtifactBridge.append()&#96; finds the existing record under the identical idempotency key, but &#96;_digest(record.payload) != _digest(new_payload)&#96; (round_number differs) trips &#96;_assert_equal()&#96;, raising &#96;ArtifactBridgeError&#96;. &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96; is called directly, not through &#96;WorkflowEngine._persist_structured&#96;'s try/except-to-&#96;WorkflowExecutionError&#96; wrapper, so this exception is not fail-closed gracefully — it propagates as a raw, undocumented crash out of &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96;, which is the very first thing the driver does on every resume. This is a landmine that fires on essentially any structured-v1-bound slice needing more than one Codex/review round, not merely a rare edge case. The only existing coverage (&#96;test_structured_bind_persists_contract_and_active_work_unit_once&#96;) calls &#96;bind_work_unit(state)&#96; twice with the SAME unchanged &#96;state&#96; object, so it cannot and does not exercise a round-number transition within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96;; the defect is unguarded by any test. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: Idempotency key for WorkUnitPayload now includes round_number (&#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;:round:&lt;n&gt;&#96;), so same-round re-binds stay idempotent and round transitions persist as a new ordered revision instead of raising ArtifactBridgeError. The exact acceptance test (test_structured_bind_survives_round_number_increase_within_same_work_unit) is present, exercises exactly the previously-broken transition, and passes in the bound 744-test attestation for the current fingerprint. Closing as originally reported by me. |
| C-05 | claude | &#96;resolve_resume_state()&#96; in &#96;src/artifact_migration.py&#96; verifies gate decisions and finding-status transitions only in the chain→mirror direction (&#96;if fact not in decisions: raise mismatch(...)&#96;, and the analogous finding-status check), unlike the attestation check (&#96;set(attestation_records) != attestation_facts&#96;) and the commit-binding check (&#96;commit_targets - bound_commit_targets&#96;), which are both symmetric. A state-v3 mirror that has advanced past the append-only record chain for a gate decision or a finding-status transition (the mirror-ahead-of-chain partial-write case this slice's own doc calls out as the central risk) is therefore accepted as a valid structured-v1 resume instead of halting with &#96;ArtifactResumeError&#96;, contradicting the documented contract that "missing... spiegelbare Fakten" must produce a resumable repair halt. No test in &#96;tests/test_artifact_migration.py&#96; exercises a mirror-side gate decision or finding transition with no chain counterpart. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: Exact acceptance test (two named regression tests) present and passing in the bound attestation; fix applies the same symmetric-set pattern already used for attestations/commit-bindings to gate decisions and finding transitions, correctly closing the mirror-ahead-of-chain gap I reported. |
| C-06 | claude | &#96;resolve_resume_state()&#96;'s quota-pause verification in &#96;src/artifact_migration.py&#96; checks only the chain→mirror direction (&#96;fact not in quota_facts&#96;) and has no symmetric check that every mirror-side quota-pause fact (&#96;unit.invocation_failures&#96; with &#96;diff_fingerprint&#96;/&#96;resume_at_utc&#96; set) has a matching &#96;QuotaPausePayload&#96; chain record, unlike the gate-decision and finding-transition checks just corrected in this same round using &#96;set(...) != set(...)&#96;. A quota pause durably written to state.json whose corresponding artifact-store append failed or was skipped is silently accepted as a valid structured-v1 resume instead of halting, letting a run bypass its intended provider-quota wait window undetected. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: The asymmetric quota-pause check is now symmetric (&#96;set(quota_records) != quota_facts&#96;), matching the pattern used for gate/finding/attestation/commit-binding checks. The exact acceptance test (&#96;test_structured_resume_halts_when_mirror_quota_pause_has_no_chain_record&#96;) is present and passes in the bound attestation for fingerprint d321fd17411b5d2fa0440f09781614671573f18618e4739f4d5b21f50c1b9f15 (755 passed full suite + isolated 1-test run, both PASS). Closing as originally reported by me. |
| C-07 | claude | The bound orchestrator validation attestation for fingerprint 1597d20e1cea83859cc382c494c08dc02f1eeaeb6de79af10d620b9bf46e13d2 is FAIL: &#96;tests/test_language_consistency.py::test_no_german_terms_in_runtime_content&#96; fails because &#96;src/artifact_projection.py&#96; (line 185, &#96;BindingPayload&#96; render branch) emits the German runtime string &#96;Freigaben&#96; inside &#96;render_artifact_sections()&#96;, violating the repository's English-only-runtime-content invariant enforced by that guard test. This Slice cannot be approved while its own new module breaks a pre-existing repository-wide invariant test, and no red-state follow-up Slice is authorized for this fingerprint. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: German runtime string "Freigaben" removed and replaced with "Approvals"; bound attestation for fingerprint 76374f1711e38301b9ce88267c749f39fc7b767f1264b05cb377fd4814a2d658 shows 764 passed / 0 failed, which includes the exact previously-failing guard test. Closing as originally reported by me. |
| C-08 | claude | &#96;ArtifactAuditProjection.selected_records&#96; in &#96;src/artifact_projection.py&#96; cannot correlate a &#96;FindingTransitionPayload&#96; record with any owning Slice/work unit (the payload carries no &#96;work_unit_id&#96;/&#96;slice_id&#96;), so every finding transition in the run is included in every per-Slice Markdown projection via the unconditional &#96;else&#96; branch, unlike &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;ReviewPayload&#96;, which are correctly scoped. This is untested for findings specifically and may be an unintended scope leak of unrelated Slices' findings into a given Slice's document, growing unboundedly as the run progresses. | OBSERVATION | angenommen | erledigt: Cross-Slice visibility of finding-transition records is now an explicit, documented contract on &#96;ArtifactAuditProjection&#96; and is covered by a concrete regression test proving exactly the intended selective behavior (findings visible, owning work-unit/review/binding records of the other Slice excluded). Closing as originally reported by me. |
| C-09 | claude | &#96;ArtifactAuditProjection.selected_records&#96; in &#96;src/artifact_projection.py&#96; scopes only &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;CorrectionWorkUnitPayload&#96; directly by &#96;slice_id&#96;, and any other payload type only via an ad-hoc &#96;getattr(payload, "work_unit_id", None)&#96; correlation to that Slice's own work-unit IDs (used for e.g. &#96;ReviewPayload&#96;). &#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;, and &#96;BindingPayload&#96; — rendered into the &#96;test-approval-premortem&#96;, &#96;validation-attestation&#96;, and &#96;approval-status&#96; sections — carry no &#96;work_unit_id&#96; field at all (none of their render branches in the same file reference &#96;payload.work_unit_id&#96;), so every one of these records unconditionally falls into the catch-all &#96;else: selected.append(record)&#96; branch and is included, unfiltered, in *every* Slice-scoped Markdown projection for the entire run, not only the intentionally global overall/work-plan projection. This directly contradicts the class docstring just added to close C-08, which states only "task, plan, and finding-transition records remain visible" globally — gates, validation attestations, and commit bindings are not in that list, yet behave identically to findings. &#96;src/orchestrator.py&#96;'s &#96;_project_audit()&#96; now calls &#96;project_structured_slice_audit()&#96; on every checkpoint in structured-v1 mode, so every Slice's persisted Markdown document will accumulate every other Slice's validation attestations, test-change gates, and commit/handoff bindings, unboundedly, for the life of the run — an unscoped leak of unrelated Slices' operational detail into every Slice's own audit trail, the exact failure mode C-08 was raised (and just closed) to prevent, but for three payload types the fix never covers. No test in &#96;tests/test_artifact_projection.py&#96; exercises Slice-exclusion for &#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, or &#96;BindingPayload&#96;; the only new regression test (&#96;test_slice_projection_accepts_chain_subsequence_and_excludes_other_work_unit&#96;) proves scoping solely for &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;ReviewPayload&#96; and intentional global scope solely for &#96;FindingTransitionPayload&#96;. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: The reported cross-slice leak for GatePayload/ValidationRequestPayload/ValidationAttestationPayload/BindingPayload is fixed: &#96;selected_records&#96; now builds &#96;slice_fingerprints&#96; from records whose payload's &#96;work_unit_id&#96; belongs to this slice's own work units, and includes any ownerless-payload record whose &#96;record.fingerprint&#96; is in that set. Hand-traced the new test: slice-6 records (work-unit-13, review-13, validation-request, validation-attestation, gate, binding — all fingerprint &#96;c*64&#96;) are excluded from the &#96;slice_id="5"&#96; projection, while &#96;_chain()&#96;'s own gate/validation/binding (fingerprint &#96;a*64&#96;, linked via that round's &#96;ReviewPayload(work_unit_id="12")&#96;) remain visible. Exact acceptance test I set is present and passes in the bound 765-test attestation. Closing as originally reported by me. |
| C-10 | claude | The C-09 fix correlates ownerless payload types (&#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;, &#96;BindingPayload&#96;) to a Slice exclusively via &#96;slice_fingerprints&#96;, and &#96;slice_fingerprints&#96; is populated only from records whose payload exposes a &#96;work_unit_id&#96; attribute — across every payload type in &#96;artifact_models&#96;/&#96;artifact_projection&#96;, that is &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; alone (&#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;CorrectionWorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;GatePayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;/&#96;BindingPayload&#96;/&#96;TaskPayload&#96;/&#96;PlanPayload&#96;/&#96;FindingTransitionPayload&#96; never populate it). Consequently, any Gate/ValidationRequest/ValidationAttestation/Binding record produced for a Slice's own active round is invisible in that Slice's own &#96;render_sections()&#96; / persisted Markdown document until a &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; carrying the identical &#96;record.fingerprint&#96; is later appended for that exact round. &#96;src/orchestrator.py::_project_audit()&#96; calls &#96;project_structured_slice_audit()&#96; on every checkpoint in structured-v1 mode, including immediately after a validation attestation and before the corresponding Claude/Antigravity review is recorded — at that checkpoint the very validation-attestation/gate records a reviewer needs are missing from the persisted Slice document, undermining exactly the "record-native view" this Slice exists to deliver and that Slice 06's cutover is meant to make authoritative. Worse: if a round's implementation is superseded (its validation fails and the work unit advances to a new round/fingerprint without ever producing a &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; for the failed round's exact fingerprint — a normal repair/retry path this same diff series already treats as legitimate, e.g. C-04/C-05/C-06), that round's Gate/Validation/Binding records can never gain a matching fingerprint and are permanently excluded from the Slice's own audit trail, even after the Slice is later approved. No test in &#96;tests/test_artifact_projection.py&#96; exercises a Gate/ValidationAttestation/Binding record for the Slice's own round before a matching-fingerprint &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; exists; the only new checkpoint-level test (&#96;test_structured_checkpoint_projects_record_chain_into_slice_and_overall_audits&#96;) only asserts a generic digest heading and a scope path, not this section's content, so the gap is unguarded. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: Structural fix anchors slice_fingerprints on AgentResultPayload/DiagnosticPayload/ReviewPayload (not ReviewPayload alone), making a round's own Gate/ValidationRequest/ValidationAttestation records visible before the first Review via the Codex AgentResultPayload's fingerprint, and keeping superseded rounds' records reachable without requiring a matching-fingerprint Review. Exact acceptance test present and passing in the bound full-suite attestation (766 passed) for fingerprint 615044a6b8d702f824be3d0d81c2213e6d8a49b933ff962885580dc2a86e10f5; existing C-09 cross-Slice exclusion tests remain green, confirming no regression. Closing as originally reported by me. |
| C-11 | claude | The protocol-mode-dependent &#96;ValidationCommand&#96; construction in &#96;_context()&#96; (&#96;argv=tuple(shlex.split(raw_command))&#96; for &#96;STRUCTURED_V1&#96; vs. &#96;shell_command=raw_command&#96; for &#96;LEGACY_STATE_V3&#96;) has no dedicated regression test in this diff and is not mentioned in the Slice-06 report's "Durchgeführte Änderungen"/"Geplante Tests" sections, even though &#96;_fresh_state&#96; now unconditionally binds every new production run to &#96;STRUCTURED_V1&#96;, making this the default path for all future &#96;--test-command&#96; overrides. Untested edge cases include quoted/multi-token &#96;--test-command&#96; values and empty/whitespace-only commands. | OBSERVATION | offen | offen |
| C-12 | claude | &#96;assert_structured_decision_context()&#96; raises &#96;WorkflowExecutionError("structured review mirror is missing its validation binding")&#96; if ANY &#96;ReviewAuditEvent&#96; across ALL persisted work-unit histories for the entire run (not only the active round) has &#96;result.validation is None&#96;; once tripped, every subsequent &#96;_agent&#96;/&#96;validate&#96;/&#96;commit_slice&#96;/&#96;finalize_audit&#96; call for the rest of the run is permanently blocked. No test in the reviewed diff constructs a resumed STRUCTURED_V1 state whose history contains a &#96;STOP_REQUESTED&#96;-style review event to confirm &#96;result.validation&#96; is always populated for stops as well as approvals/denials, so this invariant's completeness rests on an unverified assumption about every reviewer code path. | OBSERVATION | offen | offen |

| C-02 | claude | &#96;WorkflowCompletionPayload&#96;/schema &#96;workflow_completion.allOf&#96; only requires &#96;final_binding_id&#96; to be set when &#96;outcome == "completed"&#96;; it does not forbid a non-null &#96;final_binding_id&#96; for &#96;outcome in {"failed","stopped"}&#96;, permitting a logically inconsistent completion record to pass validation. | C-03 | claude | &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; can raise an exception (e.g., a transient error during the mandatory post-publish re-scan/head-cache refresh) after the record bytes are already durably and atomically published to &#96;records_dir&#96;, and this "durable-but-reported-as-failed" retry contract is exercised by one crash-recovery test but is not documented as an explicit API guarantee on &#96;put()&#96;. Slice 03 (dual-write/semantic equality) will depend on exactly this guarantee to decide whether to retry vs. treat an exception as "nothing was written," so an undocumented/underspecified contract here risks a double-write or a lost-write assumption once callers are wired in. | OBSERVATION | angenommen | erledigt: Acceptance criteria fully met in this diff: &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; now carries an explicit docstring contract ("An exception does not prove that nothing was written... callers must treat every exception as possibly already persisted and may safely retry the identical logical call"), and &#96;tests/test_artifact_store.py::test_scan_rejects_persisted_non_success_completion_with_binding&#96; directly exercises the &#96;persisted=True&#96; scan branch via a hand-written envelope that bypasses &#96;put()&#96;, matching my Slice-02 acceptance test verbatim. Closing as originally reported by me. |
| C-04 | claude | &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver._persist_structured_baseline()&#96; (invoked unconditionally, uncaught, from every &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call — i.e. every rebind/resume within a work unit) derives the &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96; idempotency key solely from &#96;unit.work_unit_id&#96; (&#96;idempotency_key=f"work-unit:{unit.work_unit_id}"&#96;), while the payload it constructs embeds &#96;round_number=unit.round_number&#96;. Elsewhere in the same diff (&#96;persist_codex_contract&#96;'s &#96;logical=f"agent-{unit.work_unit_id}-{state.current_step.value}-{unit.round_number}"&#96;, and &#96;persist_review_contract&#96;'s explicit &#96;round_number&#96; parameter used in its own idempotency key) the codebase treats &#96;round_number&#96; as a value that legitimately changes across multiple rounds within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96; (repair/retry/second-review cycles). The first &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call for round 1 durably publishes &#96;WorkUnitPayload(round_number=1,...)&#96; under key &#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;&#96;. As soon as the same work unit advances to round 2 and &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; fires again, &#96;ArtifactBridge.append()&#96; finds the existing record under the identical idempotency key, but &#96;_digest(record.payload) != _digest(new_payload)&#96; (round_number differs) trips &#96;_assert_equal()&#96;, raising &#96;ArtifactBridgeError&#96;. &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96; is called directly, not through &#96;WorkflowEngine._persist_structured&#96;'s try/except-to-&#96;WorkflowExecutionError&#96; wrapper, so this exception is not fail-closed gracefully — it propagates as a raw, undocumented crash out of &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96;, which is the very first thing the driver does on every resume. This is a landmine that fires on essentially any structured-v1-bound slice needing more than one Codex/review round, not merely a rare edge case. The only existing coverage (&#96;test_structured_bind_persists_contract_and_active_work_unit_once&#96;) calls &#96;bind_work_unit(state)&#96; twice with the SAME unchanged &#96;state&#96; object, so it cannot and does not exercise a round-number transition within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96;; the defect is unguarded by any test. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: Idempotency key for WorkUnitPayload now includes round_number (&#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;:round:&lt;n&gt;&#96;), so same-round re-binds stay idempotent and round transitions persist as a new ordered revision instead of raising ArtifactBridgeError. The exact acceptance test (test_structured_bind_survives_round_number_increase_within_same_work_unit) is present, exercises exactly the previously-broken transition, and passes in the bound 744-test attestation for the current fingerprint. Closing as originally reported by me. |

This is untested for findings specifically and may be an unintended scope leak of unrelated Slices' findings into a given Slice's document, growing unboundedly as the run progresses. | OBSERVATION | angenommen | erledigt: Cross-Slice visibility of finding-transition records is now an explicit, documented contract on &#96;ArtifactAuditProjection&#96; and is covered by a concrete regression test proving exactly the intended selective behavior (findings visible, owning work-unit/review/binding records of the other Slice excluded). Closing as originally reported by me. |
| C-09 | claude | &#96;ArtifactAuditProjection.selected_records&#96; in &#96;src/artifact_projection.py&#96; scopes only &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;CorrectionWorkUnitPayload&#96; directly by &#96;slice_id&#96;, and any other payload type only via an ad-hoc &#96;getattr(payload, "work_unit_id", None)&#96; correlation to that Slice's own work-unit IDs (used for e.g. &#96;ReviewPayload&#96;). &#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;, and &#96;BindingPayload&#96; — rendered into the &#96;test-approval-premortem&#96;, &#96;validation-attestation&#96;, and &#96;approval-status&#96; sections — carry no &#96;work_unit_id&#96; field at all (none of their render branches in the same file reference &#96;payload.work_unit_id&#96;), so every one of these records unconditionally falls into the catch-all &#96;else: selected.append(record)&#96; branch and is included, unfiltered, in *every* Slice-scoped Markdown projection for the entire run, not only the intentionally global overall/work-plan projection. This directly contradicts the class docstring just added to close C-08, which states only "task, plan, and finding-transition records remain visible" globally — gates, validation attestations, and commit bindings are not in that list, yet behave identically to findings. &#96;src/orchestrator.py&#96;'s &#96;_project_audit()&#96; now calls &#96;project_structured_slice_audit()&#96; on every checkpoint in structured-v1 mode, so every Slice's persisted Markdown document will accumulate every other Slice's validation attestations, test-change gates, and commit/handoff bindings, unboundedly, for the life of the run — an unscoped leak of unrelated Slices' operational detail into every Slice's own audit trail, the exact failure mode C-08 was raised (and just closed) to prevent, but for three payload types the fix never covers. No test in &#96;tests/test_artifact_projection.py&#96; exercises Slice-exclusion for &#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, or &#96;BindingPayload&#96;; the only new regression test (&#96;test_slice_projection_accepts_chain_subsequence_and_excludes_other_work_unit&#96;) proves scoping solely for &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;ReviewPayload&#96; and intentional global scope solely for &#96;FindingTransitionPayload&#96;. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: The reported cross-slice leak for GatePayload/ValidationRequestPayload/ValidationAttestationPayload/BindingPayload is fixed: &#96;selected_records&#96; now builds &#96;slice_fingerprints&#96; from records whose payload's &#96;work_unit_id&#96; belongs to this slice's own work units, and includes any ownerless-payload record whose &#96;record.fingerprint&#96; is in that set. Hand-traced the new test: slice-6 records (work-unit-13, review-13, validation-request, validation-attestation, gate, binding — all fingerprint &#96;c*64&#96;) are excluded from the &#96;slice_id="5"&#96; projection, while &#96;_chain()&#96;'s own gate/validation/binding (fingerprint &#96;a*64&#96;, linked via that round's &#96;ReviewPayload(work_unit_id="12")&#96;) remain visible. Exact acceptance test I set is present and passes in the bound 765-test attestation. Closing as originally reported by me. |
| C-10 | claude | The C-09 fix correlates ownerless payload types (&#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;, &#96;BindingPayload&#96;) to a Slice exclusively via &#96;slice_fingerprints&#96;, and &#96;slice_fingerprints&#96; is populated only from records whose payload exposes a &#96;work_unit_id&#96; attribute — across every payload type in &#96;artifact_models&#96;/&#96;artifact_projection&#96;, that is &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; alone (&#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;CorrectionWorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;GatePayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;/&#96;BindingPayload&#96;/&#96;TaskPayload&#96;/&#96;PlanPayload&#96;/&#96;FindingTransitionPayload&#96; never populate it). Consequently, any Gate/ValidationRequest/ValidationAttestation/Binding record produced for a Slice's own active round is invisible in that Slice's own &#96;render_sections()&#96; / persisted Markdown document until a &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; carrying the identical &#96;record.fingerprint&#96; is later appended for that exact round. &#96;src/orchestrator.py::_project_audit()&#96; calls &#96;project_structured_slice_audit()&#96; on every checkpoint in structured-v1 mode, including immediately after a validation attestation and before the corresponding Claude/Antigravity review is recorded — at that checkpoint the very validation-attestation/gate records a reviewer needs are missing from the persisted Slice document, undermining exactly the "record-native view" this Slice exists to deliver and that Slice 06's cutover is meant to make authoritative. Worse: if a round's implementation is superseded (its validation fails and the work unit advances to a new round/fingerprint without ever producing a &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; for the failed round's exact fingerprint — a normal repair/retry path this same diff series already treats as legitimate, e.g. C-04/C-05/C-06), that round's Gate/Validation/Binding records can never gain a matching fingerprint and are permanently excluded from the Slice's own audit trail, even after the Slice is later approved. No test in &#96;tests/test_artifact_projection.py&#96; exercises a Gate/ValidationAttestation/Binding record for the Slice's own round before a matching-fingerprint &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; exists; the only new checkpoint-level test (&#96;test_structured_checkpoint_projects_record_chain_into_slice_and_overall_audits&#96;) only asserts a generic digest heading and a scope path, not this section's content, so the gap is unguarded. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: Structural fix anchors slice_fingerprints on AgentResultPayload/DiagnosticPayload/ReviewPayload (not ReviewPayload alone), making a round's own Gate/ValidationRequest/ValidationAttestation records visible before the first Review via the Codex AgentResultPayload's fingerprint, and keeping superseded rounds' records reachable without requiring a matching-fingerprint Review. Exact acceptance test present and passing in the bound full-suite attestation (766 passed) for fingerprint 615044a6b8d702f824be3d0d81c2213e6d8a49b933ff962885580dc2a86e10f5; existing C-09 cross-Slice exclusion tests remain green, confirming no regression. Closing as originally reported by me. |
| C-11 | claude | The protocol-mode-dependent &#96;ValidationCommand&#96; construction in &#96;_context()&#96; (&#96;argv=tuple(shlex.split(raw_command))&#96; for &#96;STRUCTURED_V1&#96; vs. &#96;shell_command=raw_command&#96; for &#96;LEGACY_STATE_V3&#96;) has no dedicated regression test in this diff and is not mentioned in the Slice-06 report's "Durchgeführte Änderungen"/"Geplante Tests" sections, even though &#96;_fresh_state&#96; now unconditionally binds every new production run to &#96;STRUCTURED_V1&#96;, making this the default path for all future &#96;--test-command&#96; overrides. Untested edge cases include quoted/multi-token &#96;--test-command&#96; values and empty/whitespace-only commands. | OBSERVATION | angenommen | erledigt: non-issue: the STRUCTURED_V1 argv path uses &#96;shlex.split&#96;, a standard-library primitive whose quoting/tokenization semantics are independently tested upstream; the repository's own &#96;ValidationCommand.argv&#96; contract already requires list-of-strings semantics identical to &#96;shlex.split&#96; output, and no reachable code path reconstructs or reinterprets the resulting tuple. The gap is missing repo-local regression coverage/documentation, not a demonstrated functional defect; accepted as residual documentation debt in REVIEW_EVIDENCE rather than a blocking defect. |
| C-12 | claude | &#96;assert_structured_decision_context()&#96; raises &#96;WorkflowExecutionError("structured review mirror is missing its validation binding")&#96; if ANY &#96;ReviewAuditEvent&#96; across ALL persisted work-unit histories for the entire run (not only the active round) has &#96;result.validation is None&#96;; once tripped, every subsequent &#96;_agent&#96;/&#96;validate&#96;/&#96;commit_slice&#96;/&#96;finalize_audit&#96; call for the rest of the run is permanently blocked. No test in the reviewed diff constructs a resumed STRUCTURED_V1 state whose history contains a &#96;STOP_REQUESTED&#96;-style review event to confirm &#96;result.validation&#96; is always populated for stops as well as approvals/denials, so this invariant's completeness rests on an unverified assumption about every reviewer code path. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: The reordering in &#96;_run_review()&#96; now calls &#96;_record_review()&#96; (mirroring every parsed reviewer verdict, including STOP_REQUESTED) before the stop branch halts and checkpoints, closing the chain-ahead-of-mirror gap I confirmed as a real resume deadlock. Both my required acceptance signals are met: the production stop path now checkpoints a history containing the mirrored stop event (verified by the updated &#96;test_reviewer_stop_request_halts_without_contract_repair&#96; assertions), and a dedicated resume regression (&#96;test_structured_resume_accepts_mirrored_stopped_review&#96;) proves &#96;assert_structured_decision_context()&#96; no longer raises after a mirrored stop record is resumed. Full-suite attestation for the bound fingerprint (774 passed / 0 failed) includes both tests green. Closing as originally reported by me. |
| C-13 | claude | README.md/Quickstart.md now assert concrete, implementation-specific details (record filename scheme &#96;ar1-&lt;sha256&gt;.json&#96;, &#96;.orchestrator/artifacts/&lt;run-id&gt;/records/&#96; and &#96;head.json&#96; layout, immutable structured-v1 binding) that are enforced only as literal-string presence in tests/test_language_consistency.py, with no test deriving those strings from the actual ArtifactStore/artifact_migration path-construction logic (both out of this Slice's scope). A future refactor of the record filename or run-id layout could silently desync the docs from behavior while this Slice's tests keep passing. | OBSERVATION | angenommen | erledigt: non-issue for this branch: README/Quickstart claims about the &#96;ar1-&lt;sha256&gt;.json&#96; filename scheme and &#96;.orchestrator/artifacts/&lt;run-id&gt;/records/&#96; layout were hand-verified against the actual &#96;ArtifactStore._record_path&#96;/&#96;ArtifactStore.__init__&#96; implementation in this diff and match exactly; no drift exists today. The residual risk (future refactors silently desyncing docs, since the guard test only checks literal strings) is real but non-actionable for this branch and is recorded here as a residual risk rather than a new finding. |
